<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Plants">Plants</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Georgia_and_the_South_Sandwich_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Plants">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Flora_of_South_Georgia" title="Flora of South Georgia">Flora of South Georgia</a></div>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Native_plants">Native plants</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Georgia_and_the_South_Sandwich_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Native plants">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The parts of the islands that are not permanently covered in snow or ice are part of the <a href="/wiki/Scotia_Sea" title="Scotia Sea">Scotia Sea</a> Islands <a href="/wiki/Tundra" title="Tundra">tundra</a> <a href="/wiki/Ecoregion" title="Ecoregion">ecoregion</a>. In total there are 26 species of vascular plant native to South Georgia; six species of <a href="/wiki/Poaceae" title="Poaceae">grass</a>, four <a href="/wiki/Juncaceae" title="Juncaceae">rushes</a>, a single <a href="/wiki/Cyperaceae" title="Cyperaceae">sedge</a>, six <a href="/wiki/Fern" title="Fern">ferns</a>, one <a href="/wiki/Lycopodiopsida" title="Lycopodiopsida">clubmoss</a> and nine small <a href="/wiki/Forb" title="Forb">forbs</a>. There are also about 125 species of <a href="/wiki/Moss" title="Moss">moss</a>, 85 of <a href="/wiki/Marchantiophyta" title="Marchantiophyta">liverworts</a> and 150 <a href="/wiki/Lichen" title="Lichen">lichens</a>, as well as about 50 species of macrofungi.<sup id="cite_ref-Headland_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Headland-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> There are no trees or shrubs on the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The largest plant is the tussock grass <i><a href="/wiki/Poa_flabellata" title="Poa flabellata">Poa flabellata</a></i>. This grows mostly on raised beaches and steep slopes near the shore and may reach 2&#160;m (7&#160;ft). Other grasses include the tufted fescue (<i><a href="/wiki/Festuca_contracta" title="Festuca contracta">Festuca contracta</a></i>), the Alpine cat's-tail (<i><a href="/wiki/Phleum_alpinum" title="Phleum alpinum">Phleum alpinum</a></i>) and Antarctic hair-grass (<i><a href="/wiki/Deschampsia_antarctica" title="Deschampsia antarctica">Deschampsia antarctica</a></i>), and one of the most common flowering plants is the greater burnet (<i><a href="/wiki/Acaena_magellanica" title="Acaena magellanica">Acaena magellanica</a></i>).<sup id="cite_ref-Headland_33-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Headland-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Introduced_plants">Introduced plants</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Georgia_and_the_South_Sandwich_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Introduced plants">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>A number of introduced species have become naturalised; many of these were introduced by whalers in cattle fodder, and some are considered invasive.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There have been 76 introduced plant species recorded in South Georgia. 35 of these are considered eradicated, with 41 still considered present on the island. 33 of these species are planned for eradication by 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> It is considered important to control the spread of these exotic species as they readily enter this vulnerable, pristine ecosystem and outcompete populations of native flora for resources (e.g. light, nutrients) and negatively affect small, fragile habitats for the South Georgia fauna.
</p><p>Current pest plant management efforts began in the early 2000s and are primarily targeted toward the species with easier expectations of eradication in the near-term (such as bittercress and procumbent pearlwort), with remaining species to be targeted in future seasons. These programmes involved the collaboration of the South Georgia and South Sandwich Islands Government, Royal Botanical Gardens Kew, UK Darwin Initiative and private contractors.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_36-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The introduced plant species of South Georgia arrived primarily alongside human economic activities in the island and were mostly accidental, (before visitors had an understanding of their consequences). Annual meadow grass (Poa annua) is believed to have arrived approximately 1800 with the first sealers, and is now widespread across the island, particularly old sealing and whaling sites. Dandelions are believed to have been introduced alongside whaling operations, via the practice of including a handful of soil from the deceased whaler's home country. Bittercress was first spotted in 2002 and is thought to have arrived alongside building supplies at King Edward Cove. Introductions have since slowed in recent decades with the introduction of thorough biosecurity protocols. Non-native species management will require several years of regular, dedicated follow-up treatments to ensure that all germinating seed currently in the soil is controlled prior to maturity before success will be achieved.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Birds">Birds</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Georgia_and_the_South_Sandwich_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Birds">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>South Georgia supports many sea birds, including <a href="/wiki/Albatross" title="Albatross">albatross</a>, a large colony of <a href="/wiki/King_penguins" class="mw-redirect" title="King penguins">king penguins</a>, Macaroni penguins<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Penguin" title="Penguin">penguins</a> of various other species, along with <a href="/wiki/Petrel" title="Petrel">petrels</a>, <a href="/wiki/Prion_(bird)" title="Prion (bird)">prions</a>, <a href="/wiki/Shag_(bird)" class="mw-redirect" title="Shag (bird)">shags</a>, <a href="/wiki/Skua" title="Skua">skuas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gull" title="Gull">gulls</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tern" title="Tern">terns</a>. Birds unique to the archipelago are the <a href="/wiki/South_Georgia_shag" title="South Georgia shag">South Georgia shag</a>, <a href="/wiki/South_Georgia_pipit" title="South Georgia pipit">South Georgia pipit</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/South_Georgia_pintail" title="South Georgia pintail">South Georgia pintail</a>. Both South Georgia and the South Sandwich Islands have been identified as <a href="/wiki/Important_Bird_Area" title="Important Bird Area">Important Bird Areas</a> (IBA) by <a href="/wiki/BirdLife_International" title="BirdLife International">BirdLife International</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mammals">Mammals</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Georgia_and_the_South_Sandwich_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Mammals">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1134653256">.mw-parser-output .side-box{margin:4px 0;box-sizing:border-box;border:1px solid #aaa;font-size:88%;line-height:1.25em;background-color:#f9f9f9;display:flow-root}.mw-parser-output .side-box-abovebelow,.mw-parser-output .side-box-text{padding:0.25em 0.9em}.mw-parser-output .side-box-image{padding:2px 0 2px 0.9em;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .side-box-imageright{padding:2px 0.9em 2px 0;text-align:center}@media(min-width:500px){.mw-parser-output .side-box-flex{display:flex;align-items:center}.mw-parser-output .side-box-text{flex:1}}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .side-box{width:238px}.mw-parser-output .side-box-right{clear:right;float:right;margin-left:1em}.mw-parser-output .side-box-left{margin-right:1em}}</style><div class="side-box side-box-right plainlinks sistersitebox"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1126788409">.mw-parser-output .plainlist ol,.mw-parser-output .plainlist ul{line-height:inherit;list-style:none;margin:0;padding:0}.mw-parser-output .plainlist ol li,.mw-parser-output .plainlist ul li{margin-bottom:0}</style>
<div class="side-box-flex">
<div class="side-box-image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Wikispecies-logo.svg/34px-Wikispecies-logo.svg.png" decoding="async" width="34" height="40" class="noviewer" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Wikispecies-logo.svg/51px-Wikispecies-logo.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Wikispecies-logo.svg/68px-Wikispecies-logo.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="941" data-file-height="1103" /></div>
<div class="side-box-text plainlist"><a href="/wiki/Wikispecies" title="Wikispecies">Wikispecies</a> has information related to <i><b><a href="https://species.wikimedia.org/wiki/Category:South_Georgia_Island_nonmarine_fauna" class="extiw" title="wikispecies:Category:South Georgia Island nonmarine fauna">South Georgia Island nonmarine fauna</a></b></i>.</div></div>
</div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Pinniped" title="Pinniped">Seals</a> frequent the islands, and <a href="/wiki/Whale" title="Whale">whales</a> may be seen in the surrounding waters. There are no native land mammals, though <a href="/wiki/Reindeer" title="Reindeer">reindeer</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brown_rat" title="Brown rat">brown rats</a> and <a href="/wiki/House_mouse" title="House mouse">mice</a> were introduced to South Georgia through human activities.
</p><p>Rats, brought to the island as stowaways on sealing and whaling ships in the late 18th century,<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> have caused much damage to native wildlife, destroying tens of millions of ground-nesting birds' eggs and chicks. While previously the island's glaciers formed a natural barrier to the spread of rats, these glaciers are now slowly melting as the climate warms.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, scientists instituted a four-year programme to entirely eradicate the rats and mice, in what would be by far the largest rodent eradication attempt in the world to date.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> The project was led by zoologist Anthony Martin of <a href="/wiki/University_of_Dundee" title="University of Dundee">The University of Dundee</a> who stated, "This is a man-induced problem and it's about time that man put right earlier errors."<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> In July 2013, the success of the main phase of the extermination of the rats, which took place in May that year, was announced. 180 tonnes of rat poison, <a href="/wiki/Brodifacoum" title="Brodifacoum">brodifacoum</a>, were dropped over 70% of the island, in what was the world's largest ever operation of this kind.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> Another 95 tonnes of rat poison was planned to be dropped by three helicopters in January 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> In June 2015 the eradication programme concluded, apparently successfully, with the island believed "very likely" to be rat free. In 2017–18, an intensive six-month search by the South Georgia Heritage Trust, using sniffer dogs and baited traps, found no evidence of rodent presence.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Monitoring will continue for a further two or three years.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> In 2018, the number of South Georgia pipits had clearly increased.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Reindeer_in_South_Georgia" title="Reindeer in South Georgia">Reindeer were introduced to South Georgia</a> in 1911 by Norwegian whalers for meat and for sport hunting. In February 2011, the authorities announced that due to the reindeer's detrimental effect on native species and the threat of their spreading to presently pristine areas, a complete cull would take place, leading to the eradication of reindeer from the island.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> The eradication began in 2013 with 3,500 reindeer killed. Nearly all the rest were killed in early 2014, with the last (about 50) cleared in the 2014–15 southern summer.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Marine_ecosystem">Marine ecosystem</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Georgia_and_the_South_Sandwich_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Marine ecosystem">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The seas around South Georgia have a high level of <a href="/wiki/Biodiversity" title="Biodiversity">biodiversity</a>. In a recent study (2009–2011), South Georgia has been discovered to contain one of the highest levels of biodiversity among all the <a href="/wiki/Ecosystem" title="Ecosystem">ecosystems</a> on Earth.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> In respect to species, marine inhabitants endemic to this ecosystem outnumber and (in respect to biodiversity) surpass well-known regions such as the <a href="/wiki/Gal%C3%A1pagos" class="mw-redirect" title="Galápagos">Galápagos</a> or <a href="/wiki/Ecuador" title="Ecuador">Ecuador</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The marine ecosystem is thought to be vulnerable because its low temperatures mean that it can repair itself only very slowly.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> On 23 February 2012, to protect marine biodiversity, the territory's government created the South Georgia and the South Sandwich Islands Marine Protected Area&#160;&#8211;&#32;comprising 1.07&#160;million&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (410,000&#160;sq&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Georgia_and_the_South_Sandwich_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Royal_Navy_Antarctic_Patrol_Ship_HMS_Protector_MOD_45153156.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Royal_Navy_Antarctic_Patrol_Ship_HMS_Protector_MOD_45153156.jpg/220px-Royal_Navy_Antarctic_Patrol_Ship_HMS_Protector_MOD_45153156.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Royal_Navy_Antarctic_Patrol_Ship_HMS_Protector_MOD_45153156.jpg/330px-Royal_Navy_Antarctic_Patrol_Ship_HMS_Protector_MOD_45153156.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Royal_Navy_Antarctic_Patrol_Ship_HMS_Protector_MOD_45153156.jpg/440px-Royal_Navy_Antarctic_Patrol_Ship_HMS_Protector_MOD_45153156.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2708" data-file-height="1807" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Royal_Navy_Antarctic_Patrol_Ship_HMS_Protector_MOD_45153156.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>HMS <i>Protector</i></div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Military_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Military of the Falkland Islands">Military of the Falkland Islands</a></div>
<p>After the Falklands War in 1982, a full-time British military presence was maintained at <a href="/wiki/King_Edward_Point" title="King Edward Point">King Edward Point</a> on South Georgia. This was scaled down during the 1990s until the last detachment left South Georgia in March 2001, after a new station had been built and occupied by the <a href="/wiki/British_Antarctic_Survey" title="British Antarctic Survey">British Antarctic Survey</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The main British military facility in the region is at <a href="/wiki/RAF_Mount_Pleasant" title="RAF Mount Pleasant">RAF Mount Pleasant</a> and the adjacent <a href="/wiki/Mare_Harbour" title="Mare Harbour">Mare Harbour</a> naval base on <a href="/wiki/East_Falkland" title="East Falkland">East Falkland</a>, and three <a href="/wiki/Remote_Radar_Head" class="mw-redirect" title="Remote Radar Head">Remote Radar Heads</a> on the Falklands: <a href="/wiki/RRH_Mount_Kent" class="mw-redirect" title="RRH Mount Kent">RRH Mount Kent</a>, <a href="/wiki/RRH_Byron_Heights" class="mw-redirect" title="RRH Byron Heights">RRH Byron Heights</a> and <a href="/wiki/RRH_Mount_Alice" class="mw-redirect" title="RRH Mount Alice">RRH Mount Alice</a>. A handful of British naval vessels patrol the region, visiting South Georgia a few times each year and sometimes deploying small infantry patrols. Flights by RAF <a href="/wiki/Airbus_A400M" class="mw-redirect" title="Airbus A400M">Airbus A400M</a> and <a href="/wiki/Airbus_A330_MRTT" title="Airbus A330 MRTT">Airbus A330 MRTT</a> (named Atlas and Voyager by the RAF respectively) aircraft also occasionally patrol the territory.
</p><p>A Royal Navy warship carries out the <a href="/wiki/Standing_Royal_Navy_deployments" title="Standing Royal Navy deployments">Atlantic Patrol Tasking South</a> mission in the surrounding area.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/HMS_Endurance_(A171)" title="HMS Endurance (A171)">HMS&#160;<i>Endurance</i></a>, the Royal Navy ice-patrol ship, operated in the South Georgia area during part of most southern summer seasons until her near loss due to flooding in 2008. She carried out hydrological and mapping work as well as assisting with scientific fieldwork for the British Antarctic Survey, film and photographic units, and youth expedition group <a href="/wiki/BSES_Expeditions" class="mw-redirect" title="BSES Expeditions">BSES Expeditions</a>. While the final decision on the fate of <i>Endurance</i> was pending, the Royal Navy chartered a Norwegian icebreaker, renamed <a href="/wiki/HMS_Protector_(A173)" title="HMS Protector (A173)">HMS&#160;<i>Protector</i></a>, to act as replacement for three years.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> In September 2013 the British <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_(United_Kingdom)" title="Ministry of Defence (United Kingdom)">Ministry of Defence</a> purchased the ship outright.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> It was announced on 7 October 2013 that <i>Endurance</i> would be sold for scrap.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_scrapped_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_scrapped-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>

<li id="cite_note-population-2"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-population_2-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1133582631">.mw-parser-output cite.citation{font-style:inherit;word-wrap:break-word}.mw-parser-output .citation q{quotes:"\"""\"""'""'"}.mw-parser-output .citation:target{background-color:rgba(0,127,255,0.133)}.mw-parser-output .id-lock-free a,.mw-parser-output .citation .cs1-lock-free a{background:url("//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/65/Lock-green.svg")right 0.1em center/9px no-repeat}.mw-parser-output .id-lock-limited a,.mw-parser-output .id-lock-registration a,.mw-parser-output .citation .cs1-lock-limited a,.mw-parser-output .citation .cs1-lock-registration a{background:url("//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/d6/Lock-gray-alt-2.svg")right 0.1em center/9px no-repeat}.mw-parser-output .id-lock-subscription a,.mw-parser-output .citation .cs1-lock-subscription a{background:url("//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/aa/Lock-red-alt-2.svg")right 0.1em center/9px no-repeat}.mw-parser-output .cs1-ws-icon a{background:url("//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/4c/Wikisource-logo.svg")right 0.1em center/12px no-repeat}.mw-parser-output .cs1-code{color:inherit;background:inherit;border:none;padding:inherit}.mw-parser-output .cs1-hidden-error{display:none;color:#d33}.mw-parser-output .cs1-visible-error{color:#d33}.mw-parser-output .cs1-maint{display:none;color:#3a3;margin-left:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .cs1-format{font-size:95%}.mw-parser-output .cs1-kern-left{padding-left:0.2em}.mw-parser-output .cs1-kern-right{padding-right:0.2em}.mw-parser-output .citation .mw-selflink{font-weight:inherit}</style><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.gov.gs/information/about-sgssi/">"South Georgia &amp; the South Sandwich Islands – Current Status"</a>. Government of South Georgia and the South Sandwich Islands (GSGSSI)<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">31 May</span> 2016</span>. <q>There are no permanent residents in the Territory but the British Antarctic Survey (BAS) operates two bases on South Georgia. The base at King Edward Point (KEP) is operated under contract to GSGSSI and the FCO and is staffed by eight BAS personnel, plus two GSGSSI officers and their spouses. Bird Island has a year round complement of four BAS personnel who undertake long-term monitoring of seabirds and marine mammals. The South Sandwich Islands are uninhabited, though an originally undetected, and subsequently allowed, manned Argentinean research station was located on Thule from 1976 to 1982.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.btitle=South+Georgia+%26+the+South+Sandwich+Islands+%E2%80%93+Current+Status&amp;rft.pub=Government+of+South+Georgia+and+the+South+Sandwich+Islands+%28GSGSSI%29&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.gov.gs%2Finformation%2Fabout-sgssi%2F&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ASouth+Georgia+and+the+South+Sandwich+Islands" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>

<li id="cite_note-Nytimes2019-07-12-18"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Nytimes2019-07-12_18-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">

Retrieved <span class="nowrap">17 July</span> 2019</span>. <q>The area is often cloudy, and a seemingly constant volcanic plume conceals the lake most of the time. Fortunately, the team collected enough shots of the lake from 2003 to 2018 that clearly showed a crater floor containing a superheated lake 295 to 705 feet across. The lava is also 1,812 to 2,334&#160;°F (1,279&#160;°C), with the higher end of that range about as hot as lava on Earth seems to get.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=article&amp;rft.jtitle=The+New+York+Times&amp;rft.atitle=A+Burning+Lava+Lake+Concealed+by+a+Volcano%27s+Glacial+Ice&amp;rft.date=2019-07-12&amp;rft.au=Robin+George+Andrews&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.nytimes.com%2F2019%2F07%2F12%2Fscience%2Flava-lake-volcano-antarctica.html&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ASouth+Georgia+and+the+South+Sandwich+Islands" class="Z3988"></span></span>
